## Tool: exec

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"python -c @'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json,hashlib\nroot=Path('brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/notebooklm-opening-assets')\nsource_names=['2026-10-03-opening-flashcards-downloaded.json','2026-10-03-conditional-flashcards-downloaded.json']\nraw={n:hashlib.sha256((root/n).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for n in source_names}\nopening='cca0a9e6-888b-4607-b8e7-2ab94a97b0e3'\nconditional='53a08bbf-5e99-443f-bd7b-276a721cc9b0'\ncards=[]\ndef add(id,group,front,back,index,artifact=opening,note='Original example adapting the source concept; wording and reasoning reviewed.'):\n cards.append(dict(id=id,group=group,front=front,back=back,sourceArtifactId=artifact,sourceCardIndex=index,adapted=True,note=note))\nadd('foundation-enough','foundation','Every folder marked complete contains a signed form. Which condition is enough to establish the other one?','Being a folder marked complete is enough to establish that the folder contains a signed form. The rule covers every folder with that label. Sufficient goes left; necessary goes right: Folder marked complete → Contains signed form.',12)\nadd('foundation-required','foundation','Every folder marked complete contains a signed form. What is required for a folder marked complete?','It must contain a signed form. That is the necessary condition for a folder with the complete label. Necessary goes right: Folder marked complete → Contains signed form. The rule does not make every folder with a signed form complete.',17)\nadd('foundation-reverse','foundation','Every folder marked complete contains a signed form. This folder contains a signed form. Do we know that it is marked complete? Why?','No. The folder could have the signed form while other work remains. The rule tells us what a complete folder contains; it does not give the complete label to every folder containing a signed form. Keep the arrow: Folder marked complete → Contains signed form. Do not reverse it.',19)\nadd('foundation-rule-not-fact','foundation','Every folder marked complete contains a signed form. Does that sentence report that any folder is marked complete?','No. It tells us what would follow if a folder had the complete label. We need a separate fact to know whether an actual folder has that label. Folder marked complete is the sufficient condition on the left; Contains signed form is the necessary condition on the right.',2)\nadd('g1-if-first','group-1','If a visitor books a morning tour, the visitor receives a map. What complete condition does if refer to, and where does it go?','If refers to a visitor booking a morning tour. That is enough for receiving a map under this rule, so keep morning tour with booking and put the condition left: Books morning tour → Receives map.',27)\nadd('g1-if-last','group-1','A visitor receives a map if the visitor books a morning tour. Which condition goes left? Explain why.','Books a morning tour goes left. It is the if-condition even though it comes last in the sentence. Booking that tour is enough to establish the map: Books morning tour → Receives map. Sentence order has not changed the relationship.',28)\nadd('g1-when-first','group-1','When the kitchen clock chimes, the oven light switches on. Identify both conditions and place them.','The kitchen clock chiming is sufficient; the oven light switching on is necessary under this rule. Clock chimes → Oven light switches on. When refers to the clock chiming, so that condition goes left. The sentence does not say the chime caused the light.',29)\nadd('g1-when-last','group-1','The oven light switches on when the kitchen clock chimes. Does putting the light first make it the sufficient condition?','No. When still refers to the clock chiming. That is the condition that establishes the light, so it goes left: Clock chimes → Oven light switches on. The first phrase in English does not choose the arrow direction.',65)\nadd('g1-where','group-1','Where a path crosses the stream, there is a footbridge. What is enough, and what must be there under this stated rule?','A path crossing the stream is enough to establish a footbridge at that crossing. Where identifies the crossing condition, which goes left: Path crosses stream → Footbridge at crossing. A footbridge alone does not establish that a path crosses the stream there.',30)\nadd('g1-all','group-1','All notebooks on the display shelf have numbered pages. Find the complete sufficient condition.','Notebook on the display shelf is the complete sufficient condition. Notebook alone would include notebooks elsewhere, which this rule does not cover. Put the complete condition left: Notebook on display shelf → Has numbered pages.',31)\nadd('g1-every','group-1','Every student who reserves a front-row seat receives a name card. Someone writes Student → Receives name card. What did they lose?','They lost who reserves a front-row seat. The rule gives name cards to that group, not to every student. Keep the complete sufficient condition on the left: Student reserves front-row seat → Receives name card.',68)\nadd('g1-any','group-1','Any parcel collected after closing is stored in the side locker. What complete idea does any refer to?','Any refers to parcels collected after closing. Keep after closing; collecting a parcel at another time is not covered. The qualified collection condition goes left: Parcel collected after closing → Stored in side locker.',33)\nadd('g1-the-only','group-1','The only mugs on the top shelf are blue mugs. Which condition does the only identify, and where does it go?','The only refers to mugs on the top shelf. A mug in that group must be blue, so the shelf condition goes left: Mug on top shelf → Blue mug. A blue mug can be on another shelf without breaking the rule.',35)\nadd('g1-target-later','group-1','A name card is given to every student who reserves a front-row seat. Does every still identify the same sufficient condition?','Yes. Every refers to students who reserve a front-row seat. That complete condition is enough for a name card even though it appears later: Student reserves front-row seat → Receives name card. Put the condition left, not the word every.',73)\nadd('g2-only','group-2','Only visitors with a morning-tour booking receive maps. What is required for receiving a map?','Having a morning-tour booking is required. Only restricts map recipients to visitors with that booking. Put receiving a map left and the requirement right: Receives map → Has morning-tour booking. A booking alone does not establish that the visitor receives a map.',46)\nadd('g2-only-if','group-2','A visitor receives a map only if the visitor has a morning-tour booking. Which complete condition belongs on the right?','Has a morning-tour booking belongs right because it is required for receiving a map. Receives map → Has morning-tour booking. Read only if as one expression. It contains if, but it does not make the booking enough for a map.',40)\nadd('g2-if-contrast','group-2','Compare: A visitor receives a map if they book a morning tour. A visitor receives a map only if they book a morning tour. How does the booking condition change roles?','In the first sentence, booking is enough: Books morning tour → Receives map. It goes left. In the second, booking is required: Receives map → Books morning tour. It goes right. Adding only changes what the sentence promises.',41)\nadd('g2-only-when','group-2','The reading light switches on only when the desk drawer is closed. What must be true if the light is on?','The desk drawer must be closed. Only when makes a closed drawer necessary for the light: Reading light on → Desk drawer closed. A closed drawer alone does not establish that the light is on.',48)\nadd('g2-only-where','group-2','The bike racks are installed only where there is a paved area. Identify the necessary condition and place it.','Having a paved area at that location is necessary for an installed bike rack. Put it right: Bike rack installed → Paved area at that location. Only where restricts the locations with racks; it does not put a rack in every paved area.',49)\nadd('g2-always','group-2','A folder marked complete always contains a signed form. Which condition goes on each side?','Folder marked complete goes left; Contains signed form goes right. Always makes this an exceptionless claim about folders with the complete label. Read the subject and the whole relationship: Folder marked complete → Contains signed form. Always is not a rule to put whichever words come next on the right.',50)\nadd('g2-requires','group-2','Borrowing a studio microphone requires a current booking. What complete condition does requires identify?','Having a current booking is the requirement. Keep current; an expired booking would not meet it. The requirement goes right: Borrows studio microphone → Has current booking. A current booking alone does not establish that someone borrowed a microphone.',42)\nadd('g2-required-before','group-2','A current booking is required for borrowing a studio microphone. Does the first condition in this sentence go left?','No. Having a current booking is required, so it goes right even though it appears first. Borrows studio microphone → Has current booking. Ask what is required for borrowing instead of taking the words after required.',43)\nadd('g2-must-instruction','group-2','The class rule says: A student presenting today must bring a printed outline. Maya is presenting today. What is required, and do we know she brought it?','Maya is required to bring a printed outline. The instruction does not report that she did it. Preserve that meaning: Presents today → Required to bring printed outline. The requirement goes right; actual compliance needs a separate fact.',47,note='Original instruction example. Corrects the downloaded card that diagrammed required attendance as completed attendance; source cards 53 and 54 repeat that modality risk.')\nadd('g2-only-the-only','group-2','Only blue mugs are on the top shelf. The only mugs on the top shelf are blue mugs. Do these sentences give different arrows?','No. Both say a mug on the top shelf is blue: Mug on top shelf → Blue mug. Only refers to blue mugs, the required group on the right. The only refers to mugs on the top shelf, the sufficient group on the left. The complete expressions have different targets but preserve the same relationship.',45)\nadd('g2-necessary-before','group-2','A battery is necessary for this desk lamp to work. Which complete condition is required, and which condition is enough to establish it?','Having a battery is required for the lamp to work. Knowing the lamp works is enough, under this rule, to establish that it has a battery. Lamp works → Has battery. The required idea comes before necessary but still goes right. A battery alone does not establish that the lamp works.',1,artifact=conditional)\nout={'title':'Read the complete condition','reviewedDate':'2026-10-03','sourceCardIndexBase':0,'scope':['foundation','group-1','group-2'],'status':'Reviewed local teaching derivative; not a scored assessment or proof of mastery','sources':[{'artifactId':opening,'path':source_names[0],'sha256':raw[source_names[0]],'cardCount':80},{'artifactId':conditional,'path':source_names[1],'sha256':raw[source_names[1]],'cardCount':60}],'cards':cards}\nassert len(cards)==25\nassert len({c['id'] for c in cards})==len(cards)\nassert all('→' in c['back'] or 'left' in c['back'] for c in cards)\nassert all('\\u2014' not in c['front']+c['back'] for c in cards)\n(root/'2026-10-03-indicator-flashcards-reviewed.json').write_text(json.dumps(out,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\nfor n,h in raw.items(): assert hashlib.sha256((root/n).read_bytes()).hexdigest()==h\nprint(json.dumps({g:sum(c['group']==g for c in cards) for g in out['scope']}))\n'@",login:false,max_output_tokens:1200}));

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/notebooklm-opening-assets/2026-10-03-indicator-flashcards-REVIEW.md\n+# Indicator flashcards reviewed for the local opening\n+\n+Reviewed 2026-10-03. Output: `2026-10-03-indicator-flashcards-reviewed.json`.\n+\n+The two downloaded NotebookLM decks contain 80 and 60 cards. Both were read as text. The reviewed derivative has **25 cards: 4 foundation, 10 Group 1, and 11 Group 2**. Each card uses an original example or an original question about a stable example. None is an unchanged NotebookLM card. The source decks remain unchanged; their SHA-256 hashes are recorded in the derivative and were checked after writing it.\n+\n+## What the cards ask students to do\n+\n+Find the complete sufficient or necessary condition, preserve its qualifications, explain its role, and place that condition on the appropriate side of the arrow. Controlled wording pairs cover initial and final if/when clauses, only versus only if, only versus the only, and a requirement appearing before required versus after requires. Backs explain the connection in ordinary English before giving notation. Source card indices are zero-based and identify the concept adapted, not a claim that our new example was generated by NotebookLM.\n+\n+## Material corrections and exclusions\n+\n+- **Always, only, and the only:** removed list-only memorization and mechanical next-word rules. Each expression is read in a whole sentence. Always does not automatically identify whatever follows it as the necessary target.\n+- **Must and compliance:** opening cards 47, 53, and 54 blur required attendance with actual attendance. The new must card keeps an instruction's requirement distinct from a report that someone followed it. Its diagram records required action, not completed action.\n+- **Roles:** opening card 57 describes necessary as required whenever the outcome occurs, which can obscure which condition requires it. Our foundation cards explicitly name what is enough and what is required for that sufficient condition.\n+- **Rule disappearance:** conditional card 15 says the rule disappears when its trigger is false. That wording was excluded. A rule remains available; a particular fact may simply leave the other condition unsettled.\n+- **Set sizes:** conditional cards 4 and 29 imply a necessarily smaller inner set and larger outer set. They were not included. Conditional containment does not require unequal sizes.\n+- **Scope:** excluded Group 3/4, exception language, quantities, compound negation, chains, biconditionals, assumption gaps, and source exercise sentences. The cards do not ask for a contrapositive.\n+- **Voice and examples:** replaced animal categories, source names, strained examples, bare word trivia, and LaTeX fragments with small, ordinary situations and reasoned feedback. No em dashes or unsupported learning claims.\n+\n+## Review limits\n+\n+This is a reviewed teaching derivative for a local reader. It is not a scored assessment, a mastery guarantee, or evidence of learning outcomes. App rendering, keyboard reveal/reflip controls, answer visibility, responsive layout, and placement after the relevant teaching are the builder's verification responsibility. These cards presume the arrow roles have been taught before notation-based recall is assigned.\n*** End Patch"));
